## Reasoning

The champion (score 24522.85) uses:
- State 0: FlockCenter (B=10) → transitions away when few neighbours (C=4, threshold=2)
- State 1: Repulsion (B=5) → transitions on seeing red (C=7, l=1) or black floor (C=0)
- State 2: Spiral (B=6) → transitions when many neighbours (C=3, threshold=3)

This is interesting but the mission needs robots to reach the UPPER black strip (near BLUE walls). The champion seems to work somewhat but doesn't explicitly target blue walls. The key insight is:
- Blue walls (color 4 in the system? Let me check: colors are {1,2,3,4,5,6}) - we need to identify blue. Looking at the problem: BLUE walls border the rewarded strip. Color IDs: typically 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. So blue = 3.

The champion uses `--l1x0 1` (red color detection) which seems odd. But it scored best, so let's keep it and exploit around it.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Exploit: small parameter tweaks of champion
3. Explore: designs that use GoToColor(blue=3) to navigate toward the upper strip, with Stop or FlockCenter once inside

Key explorations:
- GoToColor toward blue (color 3) as primary behavior
- Use black floor detection + blue perception to stay in upper strip
- Multi-state FSMs: explore→GoToBlue→Stop

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion** (exact copy)
2. **Exploit champion**: adjust fc0, rep1, spd2 slightly
3. **Exploit champion**: adjust thresholds
4. **Exploit champion**: different probabilities
5. **Explore**: GoToColor(blue=3) + Stop when black floor detected
6. **Explore**: GoToColor(blue=3) + FlockCenter + Stop
7. **Explore**: GoToColor(blue=3) + Repulsion (to spread in upper strip) + Stop
8. **Explore**: Spiral → GoToBlue → Stop
9. **Explore**: GoAwayColor(green=2) primary
10. **Explore**: 3-state with blue perception gate
11. **Explore**: GoToColor(blue) with black floor → Stop
12. **Explore**: mix of GoToBlue and neighbor conditions
13. **Exploit #3 from best**: GoToColor(magenta) variant adapted
14. **Explore**: Simple GoToBlue → Stop 2-state

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (12176.07) is a clear breakthrough:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → if black floor (p=0.85) go to State 1 (Stop), if sees blue (p=0.9) go to State 1 (Stop)
- State 1: Stop → if random (p=0.02) go to State 0, if sees green (p=0.7) go to State 1... wait, transition goes back to itself? No - with 3 states, from state 1 the others are [0, 2]. So n1x1 D=1 targets state 2 (GoAwayColor green).
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2) → if random (p=0.3) go to State 0

So the logic is: GoToBlue → Stop when on black or seeing blue. If sees green, escape toward State 2 (GoAwayColor green). Occasionally retry from State 2. This is elegant!

**Key insight**: The robot moves toward blue walls, stops when it hits the black strip (upper), and if it detects green walls it moves away from them. The GoAwayColor(green) also helps orient toward the upper strip.

**Exploitation plan**:
1. Keep champion verbatim
2. Tune probabilities: p0x0 (black floor), p0x1 (blue perception), p1x0 (random escape), p1x1 (green detection), p2x0 (random retry)
3. Add extra transitions to refine behavior
4. Try 4-state extensions
5. Explore alternative structures

## Reasoning

The champion (8348.66) has this structure:
- **State 0**: GoToColor(blue=3) → black floor (p=0.85)→Stop, sees blue (p=0.9)→Stop
- **State 1**: Stop → random (p=0.02)→GoToBlue, sees green (p=0.7)→GoAwayGreen
- **State 2**: GoAwayColor(green=2) → random (p=0.2)→GoToBlue, black floor (p=0.7)→GoToBlue

The second-best (8914.34) is very similar but adds a random transition from State 0 with p=0.05. Third-best (9187.37) uses p0x0=0.9, p2x0=0.4, and the GoAwayGreen state also transitions on seeing blue.

Key observations:
- The winning pattern: GoToBlue → Stop (on black or blue detection) → GoAwayGreen (on green detection) → back to GoToBlue
- State 2 returning to GoToBlue on black floor (p=0.7) is crucial - this was the improvement from round 2→3
- Very small p1x0 (0.02) keeps robots stopped most of the time
- The transition from stop back to GoToBlue when seeing green (l1x1=2) is important

**Exploitation**: Fine-tune probabilities around the champion. Try p0x0 in [0.8-0.95], p0x1 in [0.85-0.95], p1x0 in [0.01-0.03], p1x1 in [0.6-0.8], p2x0 in [0.15-0.3], p2x1 in [0.6-0.8].

**Exploration**: Try adding a 4th state, try slightly different topologies where State 2 also transitions on seeing blue directly.

## Reasoning

The champion (7790.70) has:
- **State 0**: GoToColor(blue=3) → black floor (p=0.85)→Stop, sees blue (p=0.9)→Stop, sees green (p=0.7)→Stop
- **State 1**: Stop → random (p=0.02)→GoToBlue, sees green (p=0.7)→GoAwayGreen
- **State 2**: GoAwayColor(green=2) → random (p=0.2)→GoToBlue, gray floor (p=0.6? no, C=1 is gray)... wait, looking at #3 (7887.32): State 2 uses C=1 (gray floor, p=0.6) and C=0 (black floor, p=0.7). That's interesting.

Key observations from this round:
- Adding a 3rd transition to State 0 detecting green (l0x2=2, p=0.7) helped → champion
- p2x0=0.2 with random + black floor p=0.7 is the winning State 2 combo
- p1x0=0.01 is too low (worst performer), p=0.02 seems optimal
- Adding extra transition to State 1 with blue detection hurt (worst #2)
- Adding extra transition to State 2 with blue detection hurt badly (worst #3)

**Exploitation**: Focus on the champion topology. Try:
- Varying p0x2 (green detection in State 0): 0.6, 0.65, 0.75, 0.8
- Varying p2x0 (random in State 2): 0.15, 0.25
- Varying p2x1 (black floor in State 2): 0.6, 0.65, 0.75, 0.8
- Combining champion with p2x0=0.3, p2x1=0.6 (from #2)
- Try gray floor instead of black floor in State 2 (like #3)

**Exploration**: Try 4-state extension, or different State 2 behavior (GoToBlue instead of GoAwayGreen as State 2).

## Reasoning

The champion (7451.00) now uses gray floor (C=1, p=0.6) as the first transition in State 2, replacing random (C=5). This is a key insight: GoAwayGreen exits when the robot reaches the gray middle zone (p=0.6) OR the black floor (p=0.7), then returns to GoToBlue.

Key findings from round 6:
- **Gray floor (C=1) in State 2 is better than random (C=5)**: champion uses C=1 p=0.6
- #2 uses C=1 p=0.55, black p=0.65 → 7457.50 (very close)
- Adding extra transition to State 2 with blue detection was catastrophic (worst #3, score 18399)
- Adding 4th transition to State 0 hurt (worst #2)
- p1x1=0.75 (green detection in Stop) is slightly better than 0.70

- p0x2 (green in GoToBlue): 0.65, 0.7, 0.72

**Exploration**:

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
OBJECTIVE. At every simulation step the loop function adds +1 to the score for each robot that is NOT inside the target region. The score is MINIMISED, so a lower score means the swarm reached the region earlier and more robots stayed in it.

THE TARGET REGION is the upper strip of the arena. It is NOT identified by a unique floor colour: the floor is BLACK in BOTH the upper and the lower strip, and GRAY in between. Only the UPPER black strip is rewarded. Sitting on the lower black strip scores exactly as badly as standing in the middle of the arena.

HOW TO TELL THE TWO STRIPS APART. At the start of the run the arena walls light up: the two walls bordering the REWARDED upper strip are BLUE, the two walls bordering the penalised lower strip are GREEN. Colour is the only reliable cue.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FSM. A black-floor condition is useful to detect that a strip has been reached, but on its own it sends half the swarm to the wrong strip. It must be combined with colour-driven motion - move toward BLUE, or move away from GREEN - to reach the correct one. Once inside, robots should stay rather than keep exploring, since every step spent outside costs a point. Clustering tightly is NOT rewarded: only being inside counts.

# 2. THE ARENA FOR THIS RUN
The arena is a regular polygon with 6 walls, inner radius 0.81 m. 27 e-puck robots start scattered inside a disc of radius 0.53 m around the centre, with random orientations. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.41 m: neighbour-count conditions only see robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).
For reference, the published setup uses 20 robots, an inner radius of 0.87 m and a range of 0.50 m, so thresholds tuned for that setup may need adjusting here.

# 3. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 4. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 5. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 6. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
